The rule is one new pure module, `App/Plater/SlaSupportAutoPresets.{hpp,cpp}`, over the generated points alone (no model, no config, no scene): `is_sla_auto_support_base_point()` says whether a point is on the island the model stands on (an island point no more than the named `auto_support_base_layers` = 2 layers above the lowest z of the model's own mesh, in the very frame the generator returns its points in; a slope point is never the base, since it is the extra support of an overhang, and a layer height of zero gives no base rather than every point of the model as one), `sla_apply_auto_support_presets()` writes `presets.base` on those points while `support_auto_heavy_base` is on and `presets.detail` on every other generated point, and a point of type `manual_add` is skipped outright, so a support the user placed or edited keeps every value it carries. The two presets are the four `support_preset_*` values the tool already reads for its preset buttons (`SlaSupportPreset`), written onto a point by the new `apply_sla_support_preset()` in `SlaSupportPointsSettings`, which `sla_selected_support_preset_changed()` now calls instead of its own copy of those four writes. Both generation paths of the gizmo go through one new `fill_generated_point_geometry()` in place of the flat `support_geometry_defaults()` fill: the lowest z from `Biz::Algorithms::raw_mesh_bounding_box()` (MODEL PART volumes only, the very mesh the generator samples, and the lift moves model and points together), the layer height from `Domain::sla_effective_layer_height()` over the object config the M2.21 preview service resolves (0.05 mm when the object has none), and the two settings from the selected print preset, the same box the preset dimensions come from. A generated point therefore stops following the global stem and base sizes and carries the preset's own (0 mm meant "the global setting", which is what left every auto support one size before), and its head radius is the tip diameter of the preset rather than the head diameter of the tree type. Tests: the new `test/Slic3r/App/Plater/SlaSupportAutoPresetsTests.cpp` (the two keys with their category, option group, row group, tooltips and defaults; the island points of the base taking the heavy sizes while the slope point of the base layer and both detail points take the detail preset; the Mini detail; the heavy base switched off leaving every generated point with the same detail sizes, i.e. the flat placement of before; only the four preset sizes changing on a generated point; the two-layer band and the two kinds of point that are never the base; a user point keeping every value while the generated point beside it goes heavy; every detail value naming a preset of the tool), plus the two keys in `tests/sla_print/sla_invalidation_tests.cpp`. Nothing here slices: the sizes are written on the points of a generation the user asked for, and only the Slice button slices. Known gaps: the two settings are only in "Supports & raft", there is no control for them in the support tool itself, and the band is a named constant rather than a setting. NOT BUILT (a human builds and runs the tests); NOT CHECKED in a running app.
- [x] **M2.36** A "Miniature / bust" auto orient goal: a pre-supported head, a helmet or a bust is printed the way miniature printers print it (user: "will be oriented with the face of the helmet upwards to conserve as much detail as possible, and then a fat support in the neck where it will be obscured by gluing into the body and small supports to ensure all detail gets printed"). The flat face the piece is glued to a body by goes down on the plate, the detail side up so the layers cut across the face and not along it, and the piece is leaned over by a fixed angle so the cross sections ramp in from the narrow cut instead of starting at the width of the head. The rotation about Z stays what it was: the piece is not turned on the plate. "Fewest supports" is the wrong goal for such a piece, because a face exposed to print is a face with many islands on it and so with many support points - 20 of them on a 9.5 mm helmet against the 3 or 4 it came in with. · M · needs M4.11, M4.12
  Result: `AutoOrientGoal::Miniature` in `libslic3r/include/libslic3r/SLAAutoOrient.hpp`, routed to the new `sla::find_miniature_rotation()` in `SLA/Rotfinder.{hpp,cpp}`, offered in the Rotate panel as a fifth radio button, "Miniature / bust" (`RotationDialog.cpp`, with its case in `reload_auto_orient_goal()`). No config key, no new string anywhere else. **The pose is built and not searched for:** `find_glue_face()` walks the mesh once (`measure_facets()`), joins the facets that are within 2° of each other, in one plane and joined on an edge they share into planar regions (`planar_regions()`, union-find over the shared edges), and takes the biggest region of them that is under a quarter of the surface of the piece, made of more than one facet, and sitting at an extremity of the piece along its own normal with the rest of the piece behind it: the cut of a neck, the underside of a head, the flat back of a bust. With nothing flat at an extremity it takes the smallest flat region of the lowest tenth of the piece that is not looking up, and with nothing flat at all it reports no face and the piece keeps the pose it was loaded in. The lean is `miniature_tilt_degrees` (35, held between `miniature_tilt_min_degrees` and `_max_degrees`, 30..45, with the rule and its reason written over the constants): the piece is laid on its cut and tipped over about a horizontal axis, which is what makes the cross sections ramp in from the narrow cut up to the widest part of the piece instead of standing up as a full width disc. Only the azimuth of that lean is searched, 24 of them: the least area facing the plate wins, that being the area facing down (normal z < -0.5) with the cut itself left out of it, and among the azimuths that are as good as each other in that the most detail facing up, which is the surface on the far side of the cut weighted by the square root of the facet area (the weight the supportedness score of the least supports goal uses, and for the same reason: a sculpted face is a lot of small facets). Nothing is sliced and no print config is read. **The pose is built inside the two rotations of the engine instead of being taken apart again out of a quaternion:** the queued version of this todo scored a quaternion and handed out what `from_transform3f()` read back out of it, and that is `eulerAngles(2, 1, 0)` with the ends swapped, R = Rz(z) Ry(y) Rx(x); dropping the z out of that decomposition is not a spin of the piece around the plate, it is another pose - a bust whose flat back points along -Y came out with its cut 90° off the plate instead of 35°, and pointing up at all for some azimuths. `pose_pointing_glue_face_at()` now solves for the (x, y) of R = Ry(y) Rx(x) that puts the cut exactly on the direction the lean wants: Rx is the only one of the two that can move the y and the z of a normal, so n.y cos(x) - n.z sin(x) = target.y() is the whole of it, it has two roots that differ by a spin around the cut, and the one nearer to the lean that was meant is the one taken. A pose that could not be leaned that far is told from one that could (`miniature_pose_reached_deg`) and is not allowed to win, because a piece whose cut points along an axis of the plate can only be leaned to one side and the queued version had no way to say so. Tests: three new cases in `tests/sla_print/sla_rotfinder_tests.cpp`, on shapes written from scratch - a head of 8 mm on a neck of 3 mm with the neck cut flat ends with the cut 35° off the plate and the head above it and off to one side; a bust 6 x 5 mm at the cut and 20 x 14 mm at the top ends on the small face, its wide top being flat as well and rejected by the quarter-of-the-surface rule (a third of the piece); and a piece with no flat face at all falls back without throwing and is only leaned over. The ball in two of them had to become an icosphere: the quads of a latitude/longitude ball are planar (their corners are mirrored into each other, so they are two parallel lines in one plane) and the two triangles of every one of them are exactly coplanar, so such a ball carries a flat face in every quad and the "no flat face at all" case cannot be written down with it. NOT BUILT (a human builds and runs the tests in this session); NOT CHECKED in a running app.
  - [x] **M2.36b** The Miniature / bust orient goal leans 48 degrees, the median of the studio-supported miniature heads measured in doc/sla-fork/supports/calibration.md, not 35. · S · needs M2.36
    Result: `miniature_tilt_degrees` in `src/libslic3r/src/libslic3r/SLA/Rotfinder.cpp` is 48, with the measurement and the round down written over the constants, and `miniature_tilt_max_degrees` went from 45 to 60 because the clamp that holds the lean inside the range of the rule would otherwise have cut 48 back to 45: the five heads span 40.5 to 60.0 degrees off upright and the floor stays at 30. The five expectations of M2.36 in `tests/sla_print/sla_rotfinder_tests.cpp` that pin the lean (the neck cut, the bust cut, the two up axes and the fallback of the ball) pin 48 now, with every other expectation of the three cases kept. Rulebook R2.3 reads "About 48 degrees by default, the experts' median", and the line of calibration.md that offered 45-50 over the old default now says the default is this measurement rounded down. NOT BUILT: no target compiled and no test run in this session.

## M3: Resin profile import (Chitubox, Lychee and others)

**Goal:** people coming from Chitubox or Lychee can bring their resin profiles with them. They drop in the profile file, check a mapping report, and save it as a PrusaSLA material preset.

### Design

**Why a mapping report is needed**

Other slicers and PrusaSLA don't describe a resin the same way:
- Chitubox and Lychee target Z-lift MSLA printers: lift height and speed, retract, rest times, LED PWM, and a fixed-count block of bottom layers.
- Prusa SL1/SL1S use a **tilt** mechanism: `tilt_*`, `tower_*`, and settings that differ above and below `area_fill`. They also use `faded_layers`, which steps exposure down gradually (between 3 and 20 layers) instead of switching abruptly after the bottom layers.

Some values therefore map exactly, some need a unit conversion, some can only be approximated, and some can't be carried over. The importer **never loses a value silently.** Every source key ends up in the report with one of these statuses:

| Status | Meaning | UI color token |
|---|---|---|
| Exact | Copied as-is | `AccentPrimary` |
| Converted | Unit or shape changed, for example mm/min → mm/s, or per-litre price → bottle cost | `AccentSecondary` |
| Approximated | Semantics differ; a closest value was chosen | `Warning` |
| Not applicable | Doesn't apply to the target printer, for example lift settings on a tilt printer | `Text` (disabled) |
| Unknown | Key not recognized; kept only in the report | `Text` (disabled) |

**Pipeline**

```
file ──► sniff() picks a reader ──► IResinProfileReader::read()
     ──► ForeignResinProfile[]        (neutral, source-agnostic; a file may hold several)
     ──► ResinProfileMapper::map(profile, target printer, base material)
     ──► MappingResult { PresetValueMap values; vector<MappingNote> notes; conditions }
     ──► review dialog (GUI) or JSON report (CLI)
     ──► PresetInteractor::save_user_preset(...)   (user sla_material preset that inherits from the base)
```

**Code locations** (in `src/slic3r-shared/`, following the `Biz → Domain` layering)
- `include/Slic3r/Biz/Preset/Import/ForeignResinProfile.hpp`: the neutral struct. Optional fields for name, vendor, source app and version, and layer height. Bottom layer count and transition layer count. Normal and bottom versions of exposure, lift, retract, rest times and PWM. Density, price and currency unit. Printer hints: resolution, build volume, printer name. A `raw` map with every key as read.
- `.../Import/IResinProfileReader.hpp` with `ChituboxCfgReader`, `ChituboxCfgxReader`, `LycheeLyrReader`, and `SlicedArchiveResinReader` (fallback).
- `.../Import/ResinProfileMapper.hpp`: pure functions with no UI or IO. Most of the tests live here.
- `.../Import/ResinProfileImportInteractor.hpp`: ties reading, mapping and saving together, handles name collisions (`NameValidator`), and does batch import.
- App side: `App/ResinImportDialog.*`, an entry point in `MaterialSelectionDialog`, and drag-and-drop through `FileLoadingLogic`.
- CLI: `slic3r-app-cli --import-resin-profile <file> --printer <printer-id> [--base-material <id>] [--dry-run] [--report out.json]`.

**Known source formats**

| Source | File | What's known | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chitubox (v1.x) | `.cfg` | Plain text, one `key:value` per line. String values are quoted with `\n` escapes (e.g. G-code), so **split on the first colon only.** Key spellings vary by version (`bottomLayCount` / `bottomLayerCount`, `bottomLayExposureTime` / `bottomLayerExposureTime`). Newer files add two-stage lift keys (`*2`) and `resetTimeBeforeLift`/`resetTimeAfterLift`. One file mixes machine and resin settings. | Reader in M3.4 |
| Chitubox (v2) | `.cfgx` | Structure isn't publicly documented. | Inspect samples first (M3.1) |
| Lychee Slicer | `.lyr` (resin), `.lyp` (printer) | Structure isn't publicly documented. | Inspect samples first (M3.1) |
| Any slicer's sliced output | `.sl1`/`.sl1s` `config.ini` (`expTime`, `expTimeFirst`, `numFade`, …), plus other archive readers from M5 | Exposure and motion settings are embedded in the sliced file | Fallback reader M3.8. Lychee users can export one of these. |
| Resin vendor datasheet | none (typed in by hand) | Around 6 numbers: layer height, exposure, bottom exposure, bottom layers, lift height, lift speed | Quick-entry form M3.11 |

**Chitubox `.cfg` → PrusaSLA mapping**

Unit and meaning questions marked "verify" get settled in M3.1 by comparing a sample file against the Chitubox UI. Names of new keys follow M0.4.

| Chitubox key(s) | Neutral field | PrusaSLA target | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `normalExposureTime` | `exposure` | `exposure_time` | Exact |
| `bottomLayerExposureTime` \| `bottomLayExposureTime` | `bottom_exposure` | `initial_exposure_time` | Exact |
| `bottomLayerCount` \| `bottomLayCount` | `bottom_layers` | tilt printers: `resin_faded_layers`, clamped to 3–20 (the print preset's `faded_layers` only applies when the resin has none). Generic MSLA: `bottom_layer_count` | Approximated (tilt) / Exact (generic) |
| `transitionLayers`, `fadedLayers` | `transition_layers` | `resin_faded_layers`, both printer classes | Exact |
| `layerHeight` | `layer_height` | `resin_layer_height` (a resin setting since M0.4, not a preset variant condition) | Exact |
| `resinDensity` | `density` | `material_density` | Exact |
| `resinPrice` + `resinUnit` | `price_per_litre` | `bottle_cost = price × bottle_volume / 1000`, only when the unit is per litre. Currency isn't converted, which is noted. | Converted |
| `lightOffTime`, `bottomLightOffTime` | `wait_before_exposure` | `delay_before_exposure: [v, v]` (same value above and below area fill). Verify meaning. | Approximated |
| `resetTimeBeforeLift`, `resetTimeAfterLift` | `wait_before_lift`, `wait_after_lift` | tilt: `delay_after_exposure: [v, v]` for before-lift, drop after-lift. Generic: `wait_*` keys. | Approximated / Exact |
| `normalLayerLiftHeight`, `bottomLayerLiftHeight`, `*2` | `lift_height[_2]` | generic: `lift_height[_2]` and `bottom_` variants. Tilt: not applicable. | Exact / Not applicable |
| `normalLayerLiftSpeed`, `bottomLayerLiftSpeed`, `normalDropSpeed`, `*2` | `lift_speed`, `retract_speed` | generic: `lift_speed`/`retract_speed` in mm/s (source assumed mm/min; verify). Tilt: not applicable. | Converted / Not applicable |
| `normalLightIntensityPWM`, `bottomLightIntensityPWM` | `pwm` | generic: `light_pwm`. Tilt: not applicable. | Exact / Not applicable |
| `bAntiAliasing`, `antiAliasLevel`, `bImageBlur`, `minGreyLevel`, `maxGreyLevel` | raw only | not material settings; shown for information | Not applicable |
| `resolutionX/Y`, `machineWidth/Depth/Height`, `projectType`, `machineType`, `currProfile` | `printer_hints`, `name` | used to **suggest** a matching printer and a preset name; never written to the material | Converted |
| `startGcode`, `layerGcode`, `endGcode`, `displayCorrect*`, `buildAreaOffset*` | raw only | never imported | Not applicable |
| anything else | raw | report only | Unknown |

**Rules**
- **Untrusted input:** cap file size (1 MB for text; for zip-based formats, cap total uncompressed size and entry count), never evaluate G-code, handle a UTF-8 BOM, CRLF line endings, missing keys, and non-numeric values without crashing.
- **Fixtures:** write test fixtures from scratch, with key names taken from observed files. **Don't commit third-party profile files** (vendor profiles are the vendor's content). Real samples go in `local-samples/`, which is gitignored.
- **Interoperability only:** if `.cfgx` or `.lyr` turn out to be encrypted or obfuscated, **don't break the encryption.** Record that in `doc/sla-fork/formats/`, and point users to the sliced-archive fallback (M3.8) and quick entry (M3.11).
- **Traceability:** imported presets inherit from a chosen system base material for the target printer, so tilt, area-fill and other printer-specific settings stay sensible. Only the mapped values are overridden. The origin (source app, file name, import date) goes in `material_source_note`.

  Result: the counts are taken from the end-of-central-directory record before the open, which is
  the number miniz sizes its directory and its index from. `ArchiveIni.{hpp,cpp}` gained
  `ZipLimits` (max entries, max directory bytes), `ZipDirectoryInfo`, the pure
  `read_zip_directory_info(tail, file_size)` and `is_zip_file(fname)`, and `ZipReader` takes limits
  and carries the reason in `error()`; with no limits (the default, so `SL1Import` is untouched) it
  does not even read the file. The record is looked for from the end of the last 64 kB + 22 bytes
  backwards and only taken when its comment length reaches the end of the file, so a `PK\x05\x06`
  inside a comment is not mistaken for it, and a zip64 record is followed through its locator when
  the 16 bit count or the 32 bit sizes are saturated - the only way an archive can declare more than
  miniz reads. `SlicedArchiveResinReader` passes `MAX_ENTRIES_SCANNED` and the new
  `MAX_CENTRAL_DIRECTORY_SIZE` (4 MB, which 16384 entries of a job stay well under), so an archive
  over either is refused with the file, both numbers and the cap in the error and is never opened;
  the post-open entry count stays as a second line of defence. `sniff_path()` now asks
  `is_zip_file()` instead of opening the archive, which is cheaper and keeps the refusal with its
  numbers on the path the user is really on: the registry still dispatches the file to this reader,
  so the import says what was wrong with the archive rather than that nothing recognised it. Tests
  in `SlicedArchiveResinReaderTests.cpp` (2 cases, 6 sections): the record of a plain zip, a comment
  that carries a signature of its own, a zip64 record, a file that is not a zip; and the refusals -
  500000 entries in an archive of one, a 3 GB central directory, the same promise in a zip64 record,
  all three through `read()` (and the first through the registry as well, with `sniff_path()` still
  claiming the file), against an archive with a comment inside the caps that imports as before. The
  user guide's archive paragraph and its known limits carry the numbers. NOT BUILT when it was
  written; a human builds and runs the suites. Fix 2026-10-01 (found by a test build): the "more entries than the cap" case asked the record of a plain zip to declare 500000 entries, but that record counts them in 16 bits, so raw_zip() truncated the claim to 41248 and the refusal named that number, which is what the reader found. The case declares 40000 entries now: over the cap of 16384 and the most a plain record can state. The zip64 section keeps a count of 200000, since a zip64 record is the only way one that big is stated. No product change.

The report is `Biz::ResinProfile::resin_import_report_json()` (`Slic3r/Biz/ResinProfile/ResinImportReport.{hpp,cpp}`): compact JSON, fixed key order, mapping rows sorted by key, file name only, no timestamp. 5 tests in `ResinImportReportTests.cpp` pin the document of a two-file dry run, the sorting, the empty batch and a file no reader recognizes; the import fixture moved to `ResinProfileImportTestFixture.hpp` so both test files share it. - [x] **M3.11b** The datasheet form checks that a number is a positive number but not the range the option it writes allows, so a datasheet outside that range is written and shown: validate every field against the limits its `sla_material_settings` option declares and name the field that is out of range. · S · needs M3.11
  Result: `validate_datasheet()` now checks every number of the form against the `<min, max>` of the option it is written to, read out of `ConfigDefsSLA.cpp` through `Domain::get_defs_sla()` rather than repeated in the form, so a datasheet outside the range of its setting is refused before it reaches the review table instead of being written and shown there. Each rule of `ResinDatasheetForm.cpp` names the `sla_material_settings` key the mapping table writes its key to, and the value is compared **in the unit that option holds**: a rule carries how many units of the setting one unit of the form is worth, which is 1 for everything but the lift and retract speeds (asked in mm/min, written in mm/s, so divided by 60), or a limit of the setting would be applied to the wrong number. The message is a new pair, *"{field} must be {limit} or more."* and *"... or less."*, with the limit spelled the way the config spells it and the unit the option declares, so the user is told which number to change and by how much; the existing messages for a missing, non-numeric or non-positive value are untouched and still come first. A bottle below the 50 ml `bottle_volume` takes and a light-off delay above the 30 s `delay_before_exposure` takes are the two limits this build actually has, and both are now refused with the field named. The bottom layer count is checked against `bottom_layer_count`, the one a printer that lifts the build plate keeps the count in, because the 3 to 20 a tilt printer clamps it into is the mapper's doing and its row already says so. `ResinDatasheetFormTests.cpp` grows four cases: every field's option exists, is a material option and declares a minimum; the mapping table is asked where each key of the form goes and has to agree with the rule, including the price that needs `resinUnit` beside it and the bottle volume that is read but written nowhere; every field that has a maximum refuses double it with the field and the limit in the message and accepts the limit itself; and the bottle is refused below the smallest one. The field table in the user guide carries the new rules. NOT BUILT when it was written; a human builds and runs the suites. Fix 2026-10-01 (found by a test build): the table of fields in the test carried a flag for a setting the mapping table does not write, which was written as false for a bottle volume before M3.15b and is not any more, so that field was checked against an empty target key and failed. The flag is gone: every field of the form is checked against the setting its rule names, the bottle volume included, which is bottle_volume. The comment on the bottle volume rule in ResinDatasheetForm.cpp said the mapping table writes none of its own and is corrected.
  Left: only two of the eleven fields have a limit this build can act on, because `ConfigDefsSLA.cpp` gives the rest a minimum of 0 and no maximum, so a datasheet with a 400 s exposure is still accepted; the bottom layer count is only checked against the generic MSLA range, since the form does not know which printer the preset is for, and the tilt printer's own 3 to 20 clamp stays the mapper's; and a rule whose option this build does not have checks nothing, which the first new test case is what would notice.

  Result: the reverse of the M3.5/M3.6 table is `Biz/ResinProfile/ChituboxCfgExport.{hpp,cpp}`: `export_chitubox_cfg(material, printer class, name)` writes the keys `ChituboxCfgReader` reads, one "key: value" per line under a comment header, and `export_chitubox_cfg_report()` returns the same text plus a report with a row per key of the table - what was written, and for a setting Chitubox has no key for (bottle_cost, material_vendor, use_tilt, the lift keys on a tilt printer) a row that says why and is listed in `skipped`. Units go back the other way (mm/s -> mm/min for the lift, retract and drop speeds), a [below, above] area fill pair is written as the one value Chitubox has (the one below the threshold) with the other named in the note, a value of zero is not written because zero means the setting is unused, and `export_printer_class()` decides the table from the printer model and the preset's use_tilt, the same way the import does, so a preset that goes out and comes back is mapped the same way both times. `ChituboxCfgExportTests.cpp` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`) has 9 cases: the keys of a generic MSLA preset, the speeds converted back, the tilt table (no lift keys, the fade as transition layers, the wait before a lift as a delay after the exposure), the settings left out and reported, zero, the area fill pair, a preset name that cannot break the line it is on, the printer class, and the round trip: a hand-written fixture .cfg -> import (dry run mapping, and the mapper directly for the tilt class) -> export -> the reader reads it back and every mapped key comes back with the value the file gave it. On the CLI it is `--export-resin-profile <preset-name> --output <file.cfg>` next to M3.9's `--import-resin-profile` (`Init.hpp`, `ReadCLI.cpp`, `ProcessActions.cpp`): the preset is looked up by name or id among the resins of the selected printer, and the settings the file has no key for are named in the console. The loop checks what its comment says instead: a row that writes nothing carries no unit, and a row carries a unit and a unit value together. The helper reads the original first and exports under the name that file states, so the name really makes the trip; a file that states no name is still exported under the name of the round trip, and then there is nothing to compare with.

Fix 2026-10-01 (found by a test build): export_preset_to_file() opened the .cfg in text mode, so on Windows every line of the file ended in CRLF and the file on disk was not the text the report describes: the case that reads the file back and compares it with result.exported.text failed on the line endings alone. The file is written in binary now, as the .ctb, .goo and .pwmx writers and the yaml files already are, and the reader of a .cfg normalises line endings before it parses.

## M4: Engine quality (measure first; every PR includes before/after metrics)

Support generation quality has its own milestone, **M7**. M4.3–M4.5 cover regression tests and structural fixes; M7 measures quality against expert-made supports.

- [ ] **M4.1** Tracy profiling run over the benchmark set. Write a hotspot report in `doc/sla-fork/profiling/`. No code changes. · M · needs M0.13
- [ ] **M4.2** `[human]` Re-rank M4.3–M4.10 based on the M4.1 report. · S · needs M4.1
- [ ] **M4.3** Support point generation: regression tests for `tests/data/sla_islands/*.svg` and the benchmark set (PLAN B2). · M · needs M4.1
  - [x] **M4.3a** The island fixtures as a regression test; the hanging `overhang.obj` case hidden. · M · needs —
    Result: `tests/sla_print/sla_island_coverage_tests.cpp` runs the `tests/data/sla_islands/*.svg` fixtures through the pipeline; `overhang.obj` is marked as a hidden case because the support generator hangs on it. Tested: both suites green 2026-09-30.
  - [x] **M4.3b** Split the island coverage test into per-fixture cases and add a hidden local model case. · M · needs M4.3a
    Result: the test is table-driven per fixture, plus a case for a model that is kept outside the repo (the M7 dataset rule) and hidden from the default run. Tested: both suites green 2026-09-30.
  - [x] **M4.3c** Make the reported local-minimum miss measurable on the real local models: a hidden test that runs the support point generator over the benchmark corpus and lists every island (M4.8d rule) that no support point covers, with the local minima in a second list. · M · needs M4.3b
    Result: `tests/sla_print/sla_local_island_coverage_tests.cpp`, hidden `[.local]` and skipped unless `SLA_LOCAL_SAMPLES` points at the local-samples folder. It reads `<folder>/benchmark models/manifest.yaml` through the new `sla_bench_manifest.hpp` (the manifest reader, the plate placement and the path-scrubbing moved there, shared with the M0.13 harness, so both read the corpus the same way) and runs the ids of `SLA_ISLAND_IDS` (default bm01,bm13, the two helmets). Per model: placed on the plate as the benchmark does, `sla::generate_support_points_for_tool` on the default config, the model sliced with the generator's own heights, closing radius and slicing mode, and then every island of the M4.8d rule with no support point inside the coverage radius (0.5 mm in Z, sqrt(area_mm2) + 1 mm in XY) listed as id, layer index, Z, area and centroid, with a `CHECK` on the count being 0 so a miss fails visibly. The local minima are the uncovered areas the M4.8d rule does not call islands whose area grows for at least 2 layers upwards (the walk is capped at 8 layers); they are reported with INFO and as a second list, not asserted on. Only manifest ids are ever printed, never a file name or a path. **Not built or run here**, the human does it: `set SLA_LOCAL_SAMPLES=local-samples`, then `build-default\tests\sla_print\Release\sla_print_tests.exe "[.local]"`. Suspected causes of the 2026-09-28 report, from reading the generator (not changed in this job): a region whose 3D bounding sphere is under `minimal_bounding_sphere_radius` (0.2 mm, `SLA/SupportIslands/SampleConfig.hpp:36`) is dropped whole by `get_small_parts`/`erase` in `SLA/SupportPointGenerator.cpp:1042`; `move_on_mesh_surface` (`SLA/SupportPointGenerator.cpp:1609`) replaces a point with the closest mesh point when the vertical ray misses by more than one layer height, which can slide it off the region it was sampled for; the zero-elevation filter in `SLASupportTool.cpp:210` drops every point at the model's z = 0; and a region that is not an island is only sampled where `sample_overhangs` finds an overhang contour, filtered by the `min_part_length` and outline distances of `SLA/SupportIslands/SampleConfigFactory.cpp:60`.
  Left: M4.3 itself is still open: the island fixtures are covered, but the benchmark set cannot run until M4.1 (the profiling run) and M0.13 (the benchmark harness) are done.
- [ ] **M4.4** Support point generation: fix the worst unsupported-island cases found in M4.3. · L → split · needs M4.3
  - [x] **M4.4a** Measure the four suspected causes of the reported auto support miss (the list the M4.3c result note leaves behind) on a mesh built in code for each, and fix the ones that are real. · M · needs —
    Result: `tests/sla_print/sla_support_regression_tests.cpp` (registered in `tests/sla_print/CMakeLists.txt`), 5 cases on meshes written in the test, each run through `sla::generate_support_points_for_tool` on the default config at the 0.05 mm layer height of the benchmark harness and then through the island coverage rule of `sla_island_coverage_tests.cpp` (every island of the M4.8d rule, an area of at least `SLA::min_island_area_mm2` with nothing below it, has a point within 0.5 mm in Z and sqrt(area_mm2) + 1 mm in XY). The local minima of M4.3c are deliberately not asserted: a local minimum overlaps the layer below and hangs on the material under it (a valley in the outside of a dome, the bottom of a cavity), so a support point there is resin in a self supporting corner, and the list stays a list. Per cause: **(1) the small parts erase, REPRODUCED and fixed** - a 0.38 mm disc floating in mid air next to a block is collected by `get_small_parts()` (its bounding box is inside the 0.4 mm the erase looks for) and dropped whole, so the tool made no point under it at all, and the app reports the same 0.11 mm2 speck as an island that can fall off. The fix keeps a small part the island rule reports: `prepare_generator_data` filters the small parts with a new `is_reportable_island()` (an island of the M4.8d rule, so a part with empty `prev_parts`, whose area reaches one threshold) before `erase()`, and the threshold is the one the island detector already uses, moved from a local constant in `SLAPrintSteps.cpp` to `SLA::min_island_area_mm2` in `SLA/IslandDetection.hpp` so the two rules cannot drift apart. Specks under the threshold are still erased, and a small part that is *connected* to the model was never erased anyway (the flood fill of `create_small_part()` walks out of the sphere into the body), which the second case keeps that way with a 0.4 mm spike hanging under a brim. **(2) `move_on_mesh_surface()`, NOT reproduced** - a 4 x 4 mm plate floating 1.7 mm under a 30 mm brim is exactly the geometry the report describes, and the point of its first layer keeps its place: the ray down finds the plate's own bottom face 0.025 mm below, and every point in the plate's own column comes out on the plate (the brim is 1.975 mm above it). The generator can only feed this step a point that is on a surface or within a layer of one - an island point sits at a layer whose layer below is empty in its own column, an overhang sample lies on the contour it was sampled from - so the closest point branch, the one that could carry a point to another feature, is not reached; the step only ever moves a point onto its own surface. **(3) the zero elevation filter of `SLASupportTool.cpp`, NOT reproduced** - the filter drops points at the model's z = 0 within `Domain::EPSILON` (0.1 um), while a support point always sits at a layer centre, at least half a layer above the plate; a 1.5 mm plate hovering 0.5 mm above the plate with the raft around the object keeps its point. **(4) the overhang thresholds of `SampleConfigFactory`, NOT reproduced** - `min_part_length` and the outline distances belong to the island sampling, which only sees parts with nothing below them, so they cannot touch a region that is not an island; an overhang is sampled along its whole contour whatever its width, at `discretize_overhang_step` (2 mm) with a sample inside the influence radius of a point already made dropped, and a 6 mm overhang on a 14 mm plate has a point every 3 mm along its edge (unsampled, the nearest point would be 8 mm away under the post). Only cause 1 changed anything; the other three are measured, not touched. NOT BUILT, a human build and run is needed.
- [ ] **M4.5** Branching tree: reduce support volume with no new unsupported points (PLAN B3). · L → split · needs M4.1
  - [x] **M4.5a** The volume lever itself, behind a config key: how much fatter than the head it carries a branch may get, plus the volume and invariant tests on a sphere and a helmet-like dome. · M · needs —
    Result: the volume driver of the branching tree is the widening in `BranchingTreeSLA.cpp`: a node is built with `Rmin + 0.05 * pillar_widening_factor * weight`, and the weight of a node is the length of the longest chain of branches merged into it, so a trunk is as fat as the whole subtree it holds up and the pillar of a tall model is the fattest thing in the print. The new key `branchingsupport_pillar_radius_cap` (hidden like the other branching keys, `0` = off) caps that at a multiple of the radius of the head the branch carries, through `SupportTreeConfig::pillar_radius_cap`, read by `make_support_cfg` for the branching tree only. The cap is measured from the `Rmin` of the node, which is the largest head radius above it, so it can only cut the widening and never the branch below the head it serves: a cap under one is read as one and no value of the key can make a branch thinner than its head. The formula moved out of the builder class into `sla::branch_radius()` in `BranchingTreeSLA.hpp` so that a test can call it, and at 0 it is the expression of before, term for term, so the default is the tree of today. `SupportTreeBuilder` grew `diffbridges()` and `junctions()` so that a test can read the radii of the branches.
    Tests: `tests/sla_print/sla_branching_volume_tests.cpp` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`, 4 cases) writes two models in code - a 20 mm sphere standing on the plate, and a helmet-like dome, a hemisphere on a stem - puts 18 support points on the surface of each and builds every one of them twice, with the cap off and on. Per case: the fattest branch of the tree of today is above the cap (or the cap would have nothing to take out and the case would prove nothing), with the cap on no branch, pillar or junction of the tree is fatter than the cap, the resin of the tree is less (the volume of its primitives summed, which is what the cap changes), no head that had a branch loses it, none is left without one by more of them than before, and every connected head has a branch at least as fat as the head itself. The other two cases are the formula (0 is today's radius, a cap no radius reaches changes nothing, any other cap is the widening off at that factor and never below `Rmin`) and the def of the key. `sla_invalidation_tests.cpp` carries the new key in its three lists. NOT BUILT, a human build and run is needed.
    Left, and the M4.5b A/B has to know it: the tree is not the same from one run to the next, so two runs of the same model cannot be compared to the last digit. The model and bed points the tree merges branches between are drawn from `std::rand` (libigl's `random_points_on_mesh` on Eigen's generator, `BranchingTree/PointCloud.cpp`), and the per leaf ground searches run on several threads over one global NLopt generator (`Optimizer::seed` is `nlopt_srand`), so the search results race as well; the test seeds `std::rand` before each build, which fixes the first half only. Also found while reading and not changed here, both outside the volume question: `create_branching_tree` gives up on a head with `builder.head(id)` where `id` is the index of the leaf in the point cloud while `m_head_indices` is keyed by the index of the support point, so the head that is dropped is the wrong one as soon as two points are closer to each other than `non_duplicate_suppt_indices` allows or one point got no head of its own; and the cap thins the beams the tree is routed with, so a capped tree may connect heads the tree of today gave up on, which is why the cases assert that no head is lost rather than that the count is the same.
  - [ ] **M4.5b** A/B the key and the existing widening factor on the benchmark set with the M0.13 harness, record the support volume before and after, and say which default the presets get. · M · needs M4.5a, M0.13, M4.1
  - [x] **M4.5c** Make the branching tree reproducible from one run to the next, and give every head the id of the support point it was built for (the two things the M4.5a result note leaves behind). · M · needs M4.5a
    Result: the tree of one model is now the same tree twice, down to the last bit of every vertex. Three sources of run to run movement, all in the engine, and one of bookkeeping that the note found. **(1) the sampled points** - the bed and model points the tree merges branches between were drawn by libigl's `random_points_on_mesh` off Eigen's generator, which is `std::rand` (`BranchingTree/PointCloud.cpp`), so they were different in every process; the same Turk's method (a triangle with the probability of its share of the surface, then a point uniformly inside it) is drawn now from a `std::mt19937_64` seeded with an FNV-1a hash of the mesh, of the sampling radius and of which surface is sampled, so the points are a function of the input and the libigl call is gone. The uniform draw is arithmetic rather than a distribution, whose output the standard does not fix. **(2) the searches** - `Optimizer::seed()` called `nlopt_srand()`, which seeds the one process wide generator every running NLopt algorithm draws from, so the per leaf ground searches (and the pinhead and anchor searches, `SupportTreeUtils.hpp:512, 816, 997`) raced each other and their result followed the scheduling; `seed()` now only remembers the seed and `NLoptOpt::optimize()` seeds NLopt and holds a mutex (`nlopt_rng_lock()`) from the seeding to the end of `nlopt_optimize()`, so every search starts from its own seed and runs to the end before the next one starts. What is given up is the parallelism between the searches of different leaves, not inside one of them: the ray casts of a beam are still parallel. An optimizer that never asked for a seed is seeded with 0 rather than with the time, so it is reproducible too. **(3) the order the candidates are tried in** - `build_tree` sorted them by the distance to branch at with `std::sort` alone, which leaves two candidates at the same distance in whatever order the point cloud handed them over, so a tie is broken by node id now. **(4) the heads** - `calculate_pinhead_placement` sets the id of the support point it was calculated for, and `create_branching_tree` overwrote it with the number of the leaf; the leaf of a head is not that support point, since a point that gets no head of its own and a point that is dropped as the duplicate of a point next to it both move the leaves off the support points, so a head asked for by the number of a leaf is the head of another point from there on, and the tree gave up on such a head instead of on the right one. Heads are added under the id of their support point, the unroutable leaves are sorted and deduplicated before any head is given up, and a head is looked up by the id of the support point its leaf came from. `SupportTreeBuilder` keeps `m_head_indices` sparse-safe for it: a new `no_head` sentinel marks the slots of the ids no head was added under, `head_of(id)` returns nullptr for those and `has_head(id)` says so, and `head(id)` asserts on the sentinel.
    Tests: `tests/sla_print/sla_branching_determinism_tests.cpp` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`, 4 cases) writes a 20 mm sphere in code with twelve support points on its lower third, and: the same tree is built five times over and the vertex and face data of the merged support meshes are compared with the bits of the floats, so a tree that moved by a single epsilon fails and the hash in the INFO is only there to say which build moved; the tree is built once more with `tbb::global_control` pinning the process to one thread and compared with the first; the sphere is built twice more with a pair of points 0.05 mm apart added to it, of which the tree keeps one and drops the other as its duplicate (`non_duplicate_suppt_indices`), and with a point at 170 degrees of the sphere where the surface does not face down and no head fits; and every support point of that last model is asked for its head by its own id - a point that got no head has none to ask for, and the head of every other id sits where `calculate_pinhead_placement` puts it and carries that id, which is what the number of a leaf cannot say. `sla_branching_volume_tests.cpp` no longer seeds `std::rand` before a build and its note says the tree is reproducible. NOT BUILT, a human build and run is needed.
    Left: the searches of the different leaves no longer overlap in time, so a support tree is likely a little slower to build; if a run of the benchmark set shows it matters, the per leaf search is what has to be parallel again, each search with a generator of its own, which NLopt does not offer. M4.5b may now A/B the key to the last digit.
- [ ] **M4.6** Pad robustness when printing directly on the plate, plus pad generation speed (PLAN B4). · M · needs M4.1
  - [x] **M4.6a** The robustness half: pad and raft generation with the object printed directly on the plate (zero elevation, raft around the object, supports on and off), for every raft type and with the edge taper on and off. · M · needs —
    Result: `tests/sla_print/sla_pad_robustness_tests.cpp` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`) builds six awkward footprints in code - a 2 mm ring, two blocks 5 mm apart (rafts that should merge), a plate with a 3 mm slot, a 1 mm cube, a C, and a ball that touches the plate at one point - and runs each of them through the support tool (`generate_support_points_for_tool` then `build_support_tree_for_tool`) and through a full `SLAPrint` slice, both on a worker thread with a deadline, for `raft_type` None / Full / Around object / Skate, `raft_edge_taper` 0 and 1, and supports on and off. Asserted per case: no crash; a closed raft wherever one is generated (every edge shared by exactly two faces of opposite winding); the object's bottom layer inside the raft's outline and not buried in it (a raft around the object) or the raft under the supports that hold the object up (a raft under it); the elevation the object is printed at; and the documented no-raft paths - an empty raft for a raft-around-the-object type that does not follow the object, `NoPadGenerated` when a raft is required but its wall does not fit, and the cavity the pad logs and drops. Three fixes in the pad generator, all found by reading the on-plate path: `pad_blueprint` simplified the degenerate contour a slicing plane that only grazes the mesh leaves (the bottom of a ball standing on the plate is exactly such a plane), and `ExPolygon::simplify` reads the first point of the contour; `offset_contour_only` took the first piece of an inward offset that splits a raft part, which is not an outline of that part and left the raft with a hole in it, and now drops the part (the caller keeps the sharp edge or skips the part); `ConcaveHull::add_connector_rectangles` divided by the distance between an island's centroid and the centre of the islands, which is zero when two islands share one, and handed Clipper a vertex that is not a number. NOT BUILT (no build in this session).
  Progress 2026-09-30: the robustness half of M4.6 is M4.6a above; the speed half stays unticked because it needs M4.1 (the Tracy run) before any step time can be recorded. Three facts the cases had to be written around, all now covered by tests rather than changed: with the object on the plate the supports are heads on the object that reach nothing, so a raft around the object only exists with "raft around object everywhere" on - with it off every raft piece is judged redundant against support footprints that are all inside the object, and the raft comes out empty (documented, not an error); a raft *under* an object that stands on the plate has no pillars to stand on and is refused with `NoPadGenerated`, so a raft under the object asks for an elevation (5 mm in the tests); and with the default Skate values the frame around the object is as wide as the connector cavities cut into it (half the 1.6 mm expansion against a 1 mm object gap), so the cases set `pad_brim_size` to 4 mm (Skate halves it) to have a raft to test. Left: the speed half, and a decision on whether a raft around the object on the plate should need the everywhere option at all.

So the generator keeps today's behaviour, which `sla_pad_robustness_tests.cpp` already pins (a raft around an object on the plate is empty without the everywhere option, a raft under it asks for an elevation); no follow-up todo is needed. Superseded the same day: the maintainer chose Dennis Wang's raft rule instead (no raft by default, a raft where the underside would form a suction cup), rulebook R6, implemented by M7.8.4.
- [ ] **M4.7** Hollowing performance and wall thickness tolerance test (PLAN B5). · M · needs M4.1
  Progress: the test half is M4.7a below and is in; the performance half (hollowing time and peak memory over the benchmark set, before and after) is still open, because it needs the M4.1 profiling run to say what to measure.
  - [x] **M4.7a** The test half: hollow generated meshes at several `hollowing_min_thickness` / `hollowing_quality` / `hollowing_closing_distance` settings and measure the wall that comes out. · M · needs —
    Result: `tests/sla_print/sla_hollowing_tests.cpp` (2 cases, registered in `tests/sla_print/CMakeLists.txt`) hollows four models written in code — a 20mm sphere, a 20mm cube, a cylinder with a 10mm thin neck and an L block with a sharp inner corner — at 1, 2 and 3mm walls, and the sphere and the L block additionally at the accuracy extremes (quality 0 and 1, closing distance 0 and 0.5). The wall is measured by sampling the centroids of the generated interior and taking the squared distance to the outer model with `AABBMesh`; every case reports its min, mean, max, the voxel size and the tolerance with `INFO`, so a regression shows how far it moved. The bound is `min_thickness - (closing_distance + 2 * voxel)`, from the two things that can move the inner surface towards the outer one: the interior is a marching cubes extraction of the voxel SDF and so sits within about a voxel of the true offset surface, and the closing distance rounds the interior with a ball of that radius.
    A case exposed a bug and the fix is in the same commit: `mesh_to_grid` voxelizes a mesh scaled up by the voxel scale and scales the grid transform back afterwards, so the distance values stored in the grid count voxels, but `generate_interior` passed `min_thickness` and `closing_distance` to `redistance_grid` / `grid_to_mesh` and compared raw grid distances against millimetres as if they were millimetres. Since `2144f81bf1` moved the voxel scaling out of the caller and into `mesh_to_grid` the offset has been `1/voxel_scale` of the requested wall (0.4mm instead of 2mm at the same settings, 0.65mm instead of the 3mm config default), so the wall thickness was set by the quality and closing distance rather than by `hollowing_min_thickness`; the 1.1x narrow band margin, which only means something if the offset is a distance, says the same. `Hollowing.cpp` now converts the two isovalues and the band width to voxel units, divides the two grid distance getters by the voxel scale and keeps that scale in `Interior`. The min/mean/max per case are printed by the test, not recorded here: NOT BUILT, a human build and run is still needed before this half can be called green.
    Its unit conversion was reverted (M0.14-FixHollowUnits): the distance values in the grid are in world units, which was measured, so the levels this commit multiplied by the voxel scale have to be in millimetres, as they were before `5a91b6811a`. The wall this commit reported as being a fraction of the requested one was measured on a grid the same commit had already taken the levels out of.

  - [x] **M4.8e** Cup and trapped-resin detection on the sliced layers, after the slice: every region of empty space that is enclosed by solid in its layer and stays enclosed going upward until a layer closes it (a suction cup, PLAN B5b), and every cavity closed at both ends (trapped resin), each reported as a `SlaIssue{Kind::Cup}` / `SlaIssue{Kind::TrappedResin}` with its layer range, opening area and volume. No drain hole suggestions yet, that is a later sub-item. · M · needs M4.8b
    Result: `SLA/CavityDetection.{hpp,cpp}` (the PLAN B5b `SLA/CavityAnalysis.*`, named after `IslandDetection` like its neighbours) with one pure `detect_cavities(layers, layer_thicknesses_mm, opts)`, called from `merge_slices_and_eval_stats()` right after `detect_islands()` on the same merged layer polygons, so the M1.11b sidebar list (which already lists cups) and the M5.8 markers pick the kinds up; the M4.8c notification stays islands only, as its own tests require. `SLAResult.hpp` needed no edit: `Kind::Cup` and `Kind::TrappedResin` were already reserved. Definitions, in the header and as comments: a CUP is a hole of the ExPolygons of a layer that stays a hole going up until a layer covers it and has no solid under its bottom layer, so it is an upside-down pocket that pins a vacuum against the vat film on every peel; it reports the layer range, the opening area (the part of the first layer's hole with no solid under it) and the volume (hole area x layer thickness, summed). TRAPPED RESIN is a hole closed under as well, an enclosed cavity with no path to the outside in 3D, which is what a hollow print without a drain hole leaves; it reports the volume and the layer range. One walk over the layers, linear in the layer count: the holes of a layer (every hole of the layer's ExPolygons less the solid standing inside one of them, so a post in a pocket is not counted as pocket) are matched against the regions carried over from the layer below with a union-find over the overlapping ones, so a pocket that splits and merges again stays one pocket. Thresholds, both in `CavityDetectionOptions`: a cup opening under `min_cup_opening_mm2` (1 mm2) is ignored, and a pocket open below through less than that is reported as neither, too small to seal against the film and narrow enough to drain, which is how a drain hole keeps a hollow print quiet; a cavity closed on every side counts as trapped resin from `min_trapped_volume_mm3` (1 mm3) up. 13 tests in `tests/sla_print/sla_cavity_detection_tests.cpp` over generated layer geometry: an upside-down cup lifted off the plate on two uprights (64 mm2 opening, 192 mm3 over layers 1 to 3), the same cup with a vent hole through the roof (nothing), a hollow cube (216 mm3 of trapped resin), the same cube with a drain hole through its floor (nothing), a solid cube (nothing), three separate cups (three), the 1 mm2 threshold and a lowered one, the layer thickness in the volume, a post inside the pocket, a pocket that vents above, two cavities merging into one, a cavity that widens upward, and empty input. The issue note carries the numbers and `issue.layer` is the first layer of the range; the layer numbers in the notes are the 0 based slice indices, the sidebar counts them from one. Left, as the todo said: the drain hole suggestions, so no position and nothing to accept. Two limits worth knowing: the layers are merged, so a support that fills a pocket hides it (conservative, it never invents a problem), and a pocket open to the side (a notch cut from the bottom) is not a hole of a layer and is not reported. NOT BUILT, a human build and run is needed.

The floor search starts one layer above the bottom of the cavity, because that bottom is the plane the floor lies in. 7 Catch2 cases (14 with their sections) over meshes written in the test (`test/Slic3r/Biz/Sla/DrainHoleSuggestionTests.cpp`), among them a closed hollow cube answered with a hole in its bottom wall and an upside-down cup with one in its roof, a scaled and moved instance, and the nearest of two models on the axis. Nothing is sliced: the user re-slices with the Slice button. NOT BUILT, a human build and run is needed. - [x] **M4.8h** Suggest hollowing where it saves real resin: after a slice (never before, never slicing on its own), for each object that is NOT hollowed and whose cured volume is above a threshold (a new setting `hollowing_suggest_min_volume`, ml, default 20, 0 = off) and whose shape can take a wall (its smallest cross-section dimension at mid height > 2 x hollowing_min_thickness + some margin, the rule documented in the header), add a notification line: "<model> could be hollowed: about N ml of resin saved" (estimate: the volume minus the volume of a shell of hollowing_min_thickness, from the slices' areas and perimeters: area - perimeter x wall per layer), with an action "Open hollowing" that opens the hollowing tool on that model (like "Edit supports" opens the support tool). Hollowed models and FFF are skipped. Pure function for the estimate and the eligibility with Catch2 tests. · M · needs M4.8, M2.4
  Result: the App half on top of the engine half of the same commit. `App/SlaHollowingSuggestion.{hpp,cpp}` (next to `SlaIssueRows.hpp`, named for the header the engine half points at) holds the pure `hollowing_saved_ml()`, `hollowing_suggestion_eligible()`, `suggest_hollowing()`, `hollowing_settings()`, `hollowing_suggestion_text()` and `build_hollowing_suggestion_notification()`, then the `SlaHollowingNotification` listener that puts them on screen, and the new `PopNotificationType::SlaHollowingSuggestion` it closes by name. The numbers come from `SLAResultData::object_slice_stats` of the slice that just finished and from the config of that same slice (`hollowing_suggest_min_volume` and `hollowing_min_thickness` are read off it by `hollowing_settings()`, so a print sliced before a setting was changed is never judged against a value it was not made with), so nothing is ever sliced to make a suggestion: the listener runs off `StatusCode::Finished` and nothing else. THE RULE, in the header: not hollowed, a threshold above zero, at least the threshold of resin of its own, a cross section at half the height, and that cross section wider than two walls plus `hollowing_suggest_margin_mm` (1 mm, a third of the default wall and larger than the default closing distance, so a shape at the line is not one click from an unsound shell) - a wall is offset inwards from the surface on both sides of the shell, so the width it fits into is twice the wall. FFF is skipped inside `suggest_hollowing()` on the printer technology rather than in the caller, and the listener refuses a non-SLA printer before it reads a config that has neither key. THE ESTIMATE is the one the todo names, the shell of a layer being its outline times the wall, and it is an UPPER bound: two offset surfaces meeting in a corner cut away the same resin twice, so a solid 40 mm cube reads about 45 ml saved against the 17.7 ml a 3 mm shell really leaves. That is the safe direction for a suggestion (it never says a hollow print saves less than it does) and the notification says "about" for the same reason; the test pins both numbers so a change to either shows up. One notification per model, each with its own "Open hollowing" button, because the models are separate decisions and the hollowing tool takes one model at a time; the button selects the model and then activates `Scene::ToolType::SlaHollow` through the navigator, exactly the order of `SidebarSlaSupports::open_support_tool()`, and it hollows and slices nothing itself. The offers close on the same events the M4.8c issue notification closes on (a new slice starting, the project changing, the project closing), and a model that has left the plate since the slice is not offered at all, because there would be no tool to open on it. Wiring: two lines in the Plater module (the include, a forward declaration and one `make_unique`), the navigator read through the callback because the module is given one after it is built. `resources/localization/list.txt` gets the new file, or its strings never reach translators. 8 Catch2 cases (14 sections) in `test/Slic3r/App/SlaHollowingSuggestionTests.cpp` over blocks written in the test: a solid 40 mm cube (64 ml, about 45 ml saved, offered), the same cube hollowed (not offered), a 3 mm plate laid flat (4.8 ml, under the threshold, not offered), a 3 mm plate stood on edge (30 ml, over the threshold, skipped for its 3 mm cross section), a 7 mm and a 7.5 mm cross section either side of the two-walls-plus-margin line, a model with no cross section at half its height, a model under the threshold, a threshold of zero against the same cube that the default threshold offers, FFF against SLA, the text with and without a model name, the notification with its one button pressed, and the two keys read off a real `FullConfigSLA` config view and off one that has no values. NOT BUILT: a human build and run is needed. Known limit, said rather than hidden: the wall is the print-level `hollowing_min_thickness`, so a model that overrides the key for itself alone is judged by the print value.

  - [x] **M4.9b** The peel force of a layer becomes a model a user can tune instead of area times a placeholder constant: F = k_area * cured area + k_perimeter * boundary length + k_suction * the area of the cup openings M4.8e found open on that layer, with the coefficients of the vat film (`vat_film_type`, FEP / nFEP / PFA / ACF, default FEP) and optional `peel_area_coefficient` / `peel_perimeter_coefficient` overrides (0 = from the film). The coefficients and where they come from are written up in doc/sla-fork/profiling/peel-force.md, as rough figures from the literature and not as measurements. `peel_force_warning` (N, negative = the default of the film, 0 = off) turns every layer above it into a `SlaIssue` of the new kind `HighPeelForce`, and the M5.6c chart names the film in its title and writes the limit under it. · M · needs M4.9, M4.8e
    Result: `SLA/LayerStats.{hpp,cpp}` (moved from the private `src` to the public `include` of libslic3r so the app can read the model too) holds the whole model and no config reading: `peel_force_coefficients(film)`, `PeelForceSettings::coefficients()` for the two overrides, `peel_force_warning_n(setting, k)`, `layer_perimeters_mm()` (contour plus hole lengths of the same ExPolygons the area comes from), `peel_force_estimate()` and `layers_over_peel_force()`. Defaults: FEP 0.10 N/mm2 and 0.15 N/mm with a 20 N warning, nFEP 0.08 / 0.12 / 18, PFA 0.06 / 0.10 / 15, ACF 0.02 / 0.04 / 8, and k_suction 2 to 3 times k_area because a cup seals against the film. They are placeholders on the low end of the published ranges on purpose, so a layer that is really fine is not warned about. The cup term reuses the M4.8e `CupHit`s: `cup_suction_area_mm2()` in `SLA/CavityDetection.*` adds the opening of every cup to each layer it is open for (a trapped cavity adds nothing, it holds no vacuum). `merge_slices_and_eval_stats()` reads the four keys, fills `layer_peel_force` as before and adds one `HighPeelForce` issue per high layer; `SLAResult.hpp` grew one enumerator. The layer window shows the film in the chart title and, on a high layer, "Peel 24.1 N (over 20.0 N)" under it. 15 cases in `tests/sla_print/sla_layer_stats_tests.cpp` (the placeholder ones rewritten): a comb of ten 1 mm teeth costs more than the 10x10 mm square of the same 100 mm2 (220 mm of boundary against 40 mm), FEP above ACF on the same layers, every film finite and positive, the cups adding their suction term, a threshold of zero (and a negative one) flagging nothing, and a layer exactly at the limit not counted. LeastSupports uses Rotfinder's `find_least_supports_rotation`, re-added here as upstream's *on-floor* branch only, because that score (`get_supportedness_onfloor_score`) needs no print config while the other branch's `is_on_floor()` decision did; it scores the already-present `get_chull_rotations()` poses (a convex hull face laid flat), reusing the already-present `find_ground_level()`.   - [x] **M4.11b** The two objectives PLAN B7 still asks for and M4.11a did not have: least peel area (lay the object down so the biggest cross section on its way up is the smallest, the peak peel force of the print) and cup avoidance (lay it down so no layer of it is an upside-down cup), both estimated cheaply from the mesh, and both offered in the Rotate panel next to the existing goals. · M · needs M4.11a, M4.8e
    Result: the new `SLA/OrientCrossSection.{hpp,cpp}` scores one candidate pose by slicing the rotated mesh coarsely: planes 1 mm apart between the lowest and the highest vertex of the rotated mesh (between the extremes, because the slicer does not cut a horizontal face that lies exactly on a plane, and never more than one plane per 64 mm of height, so a tall pose costs no more than a short one). Two terms come out in mm²: the biggest of the slices, which is the peak peel area of `SLA/LayerStats.hpp`, and the openings of the suction cups `SLA/CavityDetection.hpp` finds on the same slices, which is the M4.8e rule run on the pose instead of on a print (a pocket enclosed in its layer that stays enclosed going up until a layer closes it), so the two goals agree with what the post-slice detection reports. `Rotfinder` drives both over the candidates the least-supports goal already uses (a convex hull face laid flat, the poses an object rests on the plate in), capped to the 24 biggest ones because slicing costs orders of magnitude more than the triangle walks of the other goals, and `find_least_peel_rotation` / `find_no_cups_rotation` sit next to the existing entries. The weights are in the header: LeastPeel is the peak area alone, NoCups is the cup openings plus 0.05 of the peak area, so a cup is only traded away for a big enough saving in peak area (5 mm² of peak area per 1 mm² of cup opening) and among the cup-free poses the one that peels easiest is still the one that is picked. Neither goal reads a print config or slices, which is what M4.11's result thought was missing. Rotfinder's searches now take a `TriangleMesh` and the `ModelObject` versions are thin wrappers over `mesh_to_rotate()`, so a caller holding no model can run them: `auto_orient_mesh(object)` takes the mesh out of the model on the UI thread and `auto_orient(mesh, goal, status)` runs the same search on it, with the progress and the cancel as before. The Rotate panel offers "Least peel" and "No cups" next to the two existing goals (the row wraps to a second line), and the search runs as a job manager job named `sla_auto_orient <project>`, which is what M4.12's result still lacks: the mesh is copied out of the model before the worker starts, the worker reports into the progress tracker, the button becomes "Cancel" while it runs and the engine is asked between the poses it scores, the rotation is applied on the main thread only if the element it was searched for is still the one selected, the one Rotate undo step is unchanged, and a pop notification (registered under `sla_auto_orient`) shows the progress when the panel is closed. Tests in `tests/sla_print/sla_rotfinder_tests.cpp`: a 60 x 40 x 2 mm plate comes back on its 40 x 2 mm edge by LeastPeel (80 mm² peak against the 2400 mm² that MinHeight lays it flat with, and much taller), an upside-down 20 mm cup (a floor and four walls around a pocket, opening on the vat) comes back with no cup and its opening off the plate, while the same cup opening up is cup free already and stays cup free, the mesh overload finds the same rotations as the model overload, a callback that refuses at once stops the search and still answers with a pose, and every search is run twice to show the answer does not depend on the order the poses came out of the parallel loop in. The coarse slices and the cups are measured in the test by slicing the mesh itself, so the engine is checked against something other than its own code. NOT BUILT: a human build and run is needed before this can be called green. Fix 2026-10-01 (found by a test build): the two goals sliced the mesh as if its vertices were millimetres, but `mesh_to_rotate()` hands them out in the scaled coordinates a model mesh is kept in, so a pose asked the slicer for a million planes per millimetre of height and a 256 mm² cup opening measured as 2.56e-10 mm², under the 1 mm² threshold of M4.8e, so the no cups goal could not see a cup at all. `score_cross_sections()` now scales the step of the planes with the Z it cuts at (and the layer thicknesses it hands the detection are in mm again), and its header no longer claims the mesh is in mm. The tests follow it: `MeshModel` builds its mesh in the coordinates an importer hands over, the measurement slices a millimetre apart in them, and the upside down cup case asks for the direction the pocket faces, which is the local -Z of the cup after the turn and not its +Z, the floor side, which would have failed as soon as the measurement found a cup.

"Auto orient" in the Rotate panel for SLA: `sla::auto_orient_min_height` (public `libslic3r/SLAAutoOrient.hpp`), applied as T(c)·R_target·R_current⁻¹·T(−c) through `transform_selection(..., place_on_bed)`, one Rotate undo step. Runs on the UI thread (a second or two); no progress/cancel yet. Not yet tried in the app.

- [ ] **M4.14** Peak memory when slicing for 12K and 16K displays (Photon Mono M5: 11520 × 5120, about 59 megapixels per layer), on an otherwise idle 8 GB machine. Record peak working set per step for a small and a tall model, and find which step holds full-resolution layer buffers. Evidence: 2026-09-22 a slice on the M5 profile ended in `std::bad_alloc`, with steps taking 3–6 minutes; a compiler build was running at the same time, so this is not yet known to be a real limit. · M · needs —
  Progress 2026-09-30: findings in `doc/sla-fork/profiling/raster-memory.md`; not ticked, the peak working set still has to be measured on a machine. The raw raster is one byte per pixel (`AGGRaster::m_buf` is a `std::vector<agg::pixfmt_gray8::pixel_type>`), so 59 MB per 12K layer. Only `slapsRasterize` ever holds them, one per layer in flight, and it ran every layer through a single unbounded `tbb::parallel_for` (granularity 1, no concurrency cap, unlike the merge step above it), so the peak was `min(cores, layers) x 59 MB` - 944 MB on 16 threads. `rasterize()` now walks the print in windows of `sla::raw_raster_batch_size()` (512 MB budget = 9 layers at 12K, clamped to the layer count and to the parallelism, so small displays and machines with 9 or fewer threads are unchanged), each rasterizer reports its own cost through the new `ISlaRasterizer::raw_raster_bytes()` (SVG reports 0), and `sla::live_raw_rasters()`/`peak_live_raw_rasters()` count the buffers. The 2026-09-22 `std::bad_alloc` was a different bug, the encoders' 59 MB per layer `reserve`, already fixed in `574213b5e1`. Also: `store_anycubic` collected the whole encoded print into a second buffer to write the layer definitions, now 36 bytes per layer instead; `store_goo` reserved 32 kB per layer and never used it, removed. Still open: the merge step keeps three copies of every layer's polygons (`m_printer_input` plus `all_layer_polygons` and `slices`), which is the larger term for a tall model, and nobody has measured the step times on 12K yet. Tests: 5 cases in `tests/sla_print/sla_raster_memory_tests.cpp` (budget arithmetic, clamps, and that a windowed loop caps the live rasters) and `src/slic3r-shared/test/Slic3r/Biz/ResultExport/SLA/SlaRasterMemoryTests.cpp`, which slices and exports a 1 mm cube at 11520 x 5120 and asserts the peak stayed inside the batch and inside the budget. Unbuilt.

On the Z side: `absolute_correction`, `elefant_foot_compensation` (ramped over `faded_layers`), then `zcorrection_layers` deleting cross-layer-bleed features from the model slices only. Three findings, none changed: the `.pwmo/.pwmx/.pwms` `antialiasing` flag and the `.goo` `anti_aliasing_level = 1` / `grey_level = 4` do not follow `gamma_correction` and understate what the encoders write, and neither can be settled without a sample file from a real printer; and the guard at the top of `apply_printer_corrections` (upstream's) skips the whole support origin, so the raft and the pillars never get a printer correction either. pm5 is correct (`PM5_LAYER_COLOR_LEVELS = 16`). The `.ctb` writer (M5.3.ctb) has an anti-aliasing flag too and it is written as 1 whatever `gamma_correction` is, so `.ctb` joined the format list in `SlaAntiAliasingTests.cpp` and is a fourth instance of the first finding; the review doc predates the writer and does not list it.
